I'm a teacher, so I know what it feels like to be drained, but this isn't the normal teacher-at-the-end-of-the-day tired. Even when I get a full night of sleep, I still have to really force myself to push through the rest of my day... grading papers, making dinner, just getting ready for the next morning. It's just a constant exhaustion.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing this persistent fatigue, even with a full night's sleep?\n\nPatient: It's been going on for about two years and two months now. It started right around the time my husband stopped working outside the home, and our whole household routine changed. I've just felt this way ever since then.\n\nDoctor: Besides the fatigue, have you noticed changes in your mood—like feeling sad, empty, or less interested in things you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. I mean, most days I can still put on a smile for my students and get through the lessons just fine. But underneath it all... there's just this gray, flat feeling. It kind of follows me home at the end of the day. And when I think about doing things I usually enjoy, like working in my garden or doing my watercolors, they just seem really far away now.\n\nDoctor: Besides the fatigue and that gray, flat feeling, have you been experiencing worry or anxiety? Do you find yourself feeling tense, restless, or on edge during your days?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I wouldn't say I feel anxious or tense. It's more just... a lack of energy, I guess. I don't feel on edge or anything like that. Just really, really tired and kind of down.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or your weight over these past two years?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed any changes there. My appetite has been pretty normal, and my weight has stayed about the same over the last couple of years. I still eat my regular meals, I just... don't really have the energy to do much else.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned you get a full night of sleep, but when you do sleep, does it feel refreshing and restorative, or do you wake up still feeling exhausted and unrested?\n\nPatient: I wake up still feeling exhausted. Even if I've slept for eight hours, it doesn't feel like it did anything. I just wake up feeling heavy and unrested, like I didn't sleep at all.\n\nDoctor: Besides feeling unrested when you wake up, do you experience any muscle aches, joint pain, or body soreness, particularly after physical activity or exertion?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really get any muscle aches or joint pain. I mean, my body just feels heavy because I'm so tired, but it doesn't actually hurt or ache. And I don't really do much physical activity anyway, honestly, because I just don't have the energy for it. So, no, no real soreness or pain.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any recent medical workup or testing—such as blood tests, thyroid function tests, or other physical examinations—to rule out any underlying medical conditions that might be causing your fatigue?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any of that done. I haven't been to my regular doctor for a check-up or any blood work recently. I just figured this was all stress or... I don't know, just me being tired. So, no tests or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned your symptoms started when your husband stopped working outside the home. Besides that change in routine, did anything else significant happen around that same time—like a loss, a major life change, or a stressful event—or was it really just the shift in your household routine that seemed to trigger this?\n\nPatient: No, nothing else really happened. It was just... he stopped working outside the home and started working from the house, and that was it. There wasn't any big loss or major stressful event or anything like that. Just that one change in our routine.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any significant illness—like a bad flu, a respiratory infection, or any other viral illness—around the time your symptoms started two years ago, or in the months leading up to when you began feeling this way?\n\nPatient: No, I didn't have any illnesses like that. No flu or infections or anything. I was physically healthy around that time, as far as I can remember.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a head injury or concussion, or experienced any headaches, dizziness, or problems with your memory or concentration since your symptoms started?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any head injuries or concussions. Um, no unusual headaches or dizziness either. As for my memory and concentration, they seem fine. I mean, I can still focus on my lesson plans and everything at school without any issues. So, no, nothing like that.\n\nDoctor: Given that your fatigue has been going on for over two years now, would you be willing to see your primary care doctor for some blood work and a physical exam to make sure there isn't a medical condition—like a thyroid problem or anemia—that could be causing this?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd be willing to do that. Um, I haven't had a physical or any blood work done in a while anyway, so it probably makes sense to just check and make sure it's nothing like that. I can call my primary care doctor's office this week and get an appointment set up.\n\nDoctor: When you wake up in the morning feeling unrested and heavy, do you also notice that you're waking up much earlier than you'd like—say, 2-3 hours before you normally would—even though you're going to bed at your usual time?\n\nPatient: No, I don't usually wake up early like that. I just sleep through the night, usually right up until my alarm goes off for school. It's just that when I do wake up, I still feel really tired, like I haven't slept at all. But I'm not waking up hours before I need to.\n\nDoctor: In the months before your symptoms started two years ago, did you have any vaccinations, start any new medications, or experience any other medical events or procedures—even minor ones—that you might not have thought of as significant?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I hadn't started any new medications or had any vaccines around that time. And no procedures or anything either. I've been pretty healthy medically, so there wasn't really anything going on right before this all started.\n\nDoctor: Besides the fatigue and the gray, flat mood you described, have you noticed any fever, chills, night sweats, or any other symptoms that come and go—or have you felt generally unwell in your body in any way over these past two years?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. No fevers or chills or anything. Honestly, physically, besides just feeling tired and heavy all the time, I've felt fine. There haven't been any night sweats or feeling sick in my body. It's really just the fatigue and the mood.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It started right around the time my husband stopped working outside the home, and our whole household routine changed. I've just felt this way ever since then.\n\nDoctor: Besides the fatigue, have you noticed changes in your mood—like feeling sad, empty, or less interested in things you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. I mean, most days I can still put on a smile for my students and get through the lessons just fine. But underneath it all... there's just this gray, flat feeling. It kind of follows me home at the end of the day. And when I think about doing things I usually enjoy, like working in my garden or doing my watercolors, they just seem really far away now.\n\nDoctor: Besides the fatigue and that gray, flat feeling, have you been experiencing worry or anxiety? Do you find yourself feeling tense, restless, or on edge during your days?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I wouldn't say I feel anxious or tense. It's more just... a lack of energy, I guess. I don't feel on edge or anything like that. Just really, really tired and kind of down.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or your weight over these past two years?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed any changes there. My appetite has been pretty normal, and my weight has stayed about the same over the last couple of years. I still eat my regular meals, I just... don't really have the energy to do much else.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned you get a full night of sleep, but when you do sleep, does it feel refreshing and restorative, or do you wake up still feeling exhausted and unrested?\n\nPatient: I wake up still feeling exhausted. Even if I've slept for eight hours, it doesn't feel like it did anything. I just wake up feeling heavy and unrested, like I didn't sleep at all.\n\nDoctor: Besides feeling unrested when you wake up, do you experience any muscle aches, joint pain, or body soreness, particularly after physical activity or exertion?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really get any muscle aches or joint pain. I mean, my body just feels heavy because I'm so tired, but it doesn't actually hurt or ache. And I don't really do much physical activity anyway, honestly, because I just don't have the energy for it. So, no, no real soreness or pain.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any recent medical workup or testing—such as blood tests, thyroid function tests, or other physical examinations—to rule out any underlying medical conditions that might be causing your fatigue?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any of that done. I haven't been to my regular doctor for a check-up or any blood work recently. I just figured this was all stress or... I don't know, just me being tired. So, no tests or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned your symptoms started when your husband stopped working outside the home. Besides that change in routine, did anything else significant happen around that same time—like a loss, a major life change, or a stressful event—or was it really just the shift in your household routine that seemed to trigger this?\n\nPatient: No, nothing else really happened. It was just... he stopped working outside the home and started working from the house, and that was it. There wasn't any big loss or major stressful event or anything like that. Just that one change in our routine.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any significant illness—like a bad flu, a respiratory infection, or any other viral illness—around the time your symptoms started two years ago, or in the months leading up to when you began feeling this way?\n\nPatient: No, I didn't have any illnesses like that. No flu or infections or anything. I was physically healthy around that time, as far as I can remember.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a head injury or concussion, or experienced any headaches, dizziness, or problems with your memory or concentration since your symptoms started?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any head injuries or concussions. Um, no unusual headaches or dizziness either. As for my memory and concentration, they seem fine. I mean, I can still focus on my lesson plans and everything at school without any issues. So, no, nothing like that.\n\nDoctor: Given that your fatigue has been going on for over two years now, would you be willing to see your primary care doctor for some blood work and a physical exam to make sure there isn't a medical condition—like a thyroid problem or anemia—that could be causing this?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd be willing to do that. Um, I haven't had a physical or any blood work done in a while anyway, so it probably makes sense to just check and make sure it's nothing like that. I can call my primary care doctor's office this week and get an appointment set up.\n\nDoctor: When you wake up in the morning feeling unrested and heavy, do you also notice that you're waking up much earlier than you'd like—say, 2-3 hours before you normally would—even though you're going to bed at your usual time?\n\nPatient: No, I don't usually wake up early like that. I just sleep through the night, usually right up until my alarm goes off for school. It's just that when I do wake up, I still feel really tired, like I haven't slept at all. But I'm not waking up hours before I need to.\n\nDoctor: In the months before your symptoms started two years ago, did you have any vaccinations, start any new medications, or experience any other medical events or procedures—even minor ones—that you might not have thought of as significant?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I hadn't started any new medications or had any vaccines around that time. And no procedures or anything either. I've been pretty healthy medically, so there wasn't really anything going on right before this all started.\n\nDoctor: Besides the fatigue and the gray, flat mood you described, have you noticed any fever, chills, night sweats, or any other symptoms that come and go—or have you felt generally unwell in your body in any way over these past two years?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. No fevers or chills or anything. Honestly, physically, besides just feeling tired and heavy all the time, I've felt fine. There haven't been any night sweats or feeling sick in my body. It's really just the fatigue and the mood.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulty with your thinking, like trouble organizing your thoughts, making decisions, or finding the right words—anything that feels different from how your mind normally works?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I mean, my thinking feels pretty normal. I don't have trouble organizing my thoughts or finding words. I can still plan out my lessons and grade papers just fine. It's just... I just don't have the energy or the desire to do it, you know? But my mind itself seems to be working okay.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any unexplained weight loss or gain, changes in how much you're eating, or any digestive problems like constipation or diarrhea over these past two years?\n\nPatient: No, my weight has stayed pretty much the same. I haven't noticed any big changes in my appetite, either. I eat about what I usually do. And no stomach issues or anything like that... my digestion has been totally normal.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned you'd be willing to see your primary care doctor for blood work. Before we wrap up today, I want to make sure we're not missing anything—has your doctor ever suggested that your fatigue and low mood could be related to a medical condition like thyroid disease, vitamin deficiency, sleep apnea, or anything else physical?\n\nPatient: No, they haven't. I mean, I haven't really brought how I've been feeling up with my regular doctor before, so we haven't really looked into it. I usually just go in for my normal yearly check-ups, and they've never mentioned anything about my thyroid or vitamins or anything like that being an issue. So, no, no one has ever suggested it was tied to a physical condition.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any recent blood work, imaging studies, or medical evaluation that might have identified an underlying medical condition—such as thyroid problems, vitamin B12 deficiency, anemia, or anything else—that could be causing your fatigue and mood symptoms?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any recent blood work or tests like that. Like I said, I haven't really talked to my doctor about how I've been feeling, so they haven't run any specific tests for my thyroid or vitamins or anything. I just haven't had any of that checked lately.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
